Use `tools/fleet-health.sh --fix` as cron (every 6h) for auto-healing.\n149:<!-- meta: domain=proxmox,fleet | severity=medium | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-03-02 | keywords=start-claude.sh,set -e,pipefail,non-fatal,best-effort,NFS mount,checkpoint,auth check,wrksplogin -->\n158:<!-- meta: domain=proxmox,fleet | severity=high | tier=active | triggers=2 | last_triggered=2026-03-02 | keywords=session-start,sign-directive,directive signing,watchdog,NFS hooks,SessionStart,UserPromptSubmit,verify-directive -->\n172:### Fleet Shared Context Pattern (2026-03-02) {#fleet-shared-context-pattern}\n173:<!-- meta: domain=proxmox,fleet | severity=medium | tier=active | triggers=7 | last_triggered=2026-03-02 | keywords=shared context,source material,agent fleet,drift prevention,parallel agents,spec document,phase 1,phase 2,orchestration -->\n174:- All agents must read the same shared source material (spec, vision doc, requirements) before producing role-specific outputs — prevents divergent assumptions across the fleet"}]},"uuid":"e693e8a0-fb22-4f44-ac92-17f171b2ed89","timestamp":"2026-03-13T07:38:10.409Z","toolUseResult":{"mode":"content","numFiles":0,"filenames":[],"content":"9:<!-- meta: domain=proxmox,network | severity=high | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-02-22 | keywords=ssh,proxmox_management,id_ed25519,gpu,pve,ssh-add,guest agent,596,agent fleet,ubuntu -->\n15:- Agent fleet VMs use `proxmox_management` key with user `ubuntu`: `ssh -i ~/.ssh/proxmox_management ubuntu@<IP>`\n38:The Proxmox API token ID `root@pam!claude-fleet` contains `!` which curl cannot send correctly even in single-quoted shell strings. The `!` gets escaped to `\\!` by zsh history expansion, causing 401 errors.\n46:token = 'PVEAPIToken=root@pam!claude-fleet=999b2ef4-c70c-47bb-8385-61f931005344'\n77:<!-- meta: domain=proxmox,discord | severity=medium | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-03-02 | keywords=claude.json,hasCompletedOnboarding,onboarding wizard,headless,browser,settings.local.json,fleet,theme -->\n94:**For fleet-wide fix**: Loop over all VM IPs and apply the same patch.\n127:### Fleet Auth: Env Var Overrides Credential File (2026-03-02) {#fleet-auth-env-var-override}\n128:<!-- meta: domain=proxmox,fleet | severity=critical | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-03-02 | keywords=CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN,/etc/claude-code.env,credentials.json,fleet-reauth,keychain,OAuth,expired token,env var override,fleet-health -->\n132:- All 7 fleet VMs had a stale OAuth token baked into `/etc/claude-code.env` that was sourced on every login shell\n135:- **Prevention**: Use `tools/fleet-reauth.sh` to deploy credentials from Mac Keychain. Use `tools/fleet-health.sh --fix` as cron (every 6h) for auto-healing.\n149:<!-- meta: domain=proxmox,fleet | severity=medium | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-03-02 | keywords=start-claude.sh,set -e,pipefail,non-fatal,best-effort,NFS mount,checkpoint,auth check,wrksplogin -->\n158:<!-- meta: domain=proxmox,fleet | severity=high | tier=active | triggers=2 | last_triggered=2026-03-02 | keywords=session-start,sign-directive,directive signing,watchdog,NFS hooks,SessionStart,UserPromptSubmit,verify-directive -->\n172:### Fleet Shared Context Pattern (2026-03-02) {#fleet-shared-context-pattern}\n173:<!-- meta: domain=proxmox,fleet | severity=medium | tier=active | triggers=7 | last_triggered=2026-03-02 | keywords=shared context,source material,agent fleet,drift prevention,parallel agents,spec document,phase 1,phase 2,orchestration -->\n174:- All agents must read the same shared source material (spec, vision doc, requirements) before producing role-specific outputs — prevents divergent assumptions across the fleet","numLines":15},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"76565899-ebca-43bd-aa73-d92575660ff6"}
/Users/eviemini/.claude/projects/-Users-eviemini-Documents-code-server-manager/02610c2a-bd14-4804-96cd-a256bf0aa29c.jsonl:{"parentUuid":"7b0bf795-f3ac-418e-8804-75afe5444a98","isSidechain":false,"userType":"external","cwd":"/Users/eviemini/Documents/code/server-manager","sessionId":"02610c2a-bd14-4804-96cd-a256bf0aa29c","version":"2.1.71","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"iterative-crafting-cat","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01RszPk7js3jQbxNKj4swipW","type":"tool_result","content":"The file /Users/eviemini/Documents/code/server-manager/memory/lessons/proxmox.md has been updated successfully."}]},"uuid":"7c9384f8-3ca8-48d7-a21e-985da9fbc546","timestamp":"2026-03-13T07:38:22.491Z","toolUseResult":{"filePath":"/Users/eviemini/Documents/code/server-manager/memory/lessons/proxmox.md","oldString":"### EnterWorktree Tool (2026-02-21) {#enter-worktree-tool}","newString":"### Fleet Dispatch vs Local Subagents (2026-03-09) {#fleet-dispatch-vs-local}\n<!-- meta: domain=proxmox,fleet | severity=medium | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-03-09 | keywords=fleet dispatch,local agent,background agent,nod queue,SSH,VM ops,subagent -->\n- For ops tasks targeting specific VMs (media monitoring, service fixes, cron debugging), dispatch to the fleet via nod queue or direct SSH — not local background agents\n- Local subagents can't SSH into VMs, lack credentials, and duplicate work the orchestrator ends up doing anyway\n- **Rule**: Local subagents = research/analysis only. Fleet dispatch = anything that touches a VM or service\n- The fleet exists for a reason — use it\n\n### EnterWorktree Tool (2026-02-21) {#enter-worktree-tool}","originalFile":"# Lessons — Proxmox & VMs\n\n### LXC Containers (2026-02-18) {#lxc-containers-broken}\n<!-- meta: domain=proxmox,lxc | severity=high | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-02-18 | keywords=lxc,qemu,api exec,ssh,ci-runner,container,vm,132 -->\n- LXC containers have broken API exec and no SSH — always use QEMU VMs\n- Deleted ci-runner-4 (ID 132) for this reason\n\n### Proxmox SSH (2026-02-22) {#proxmox-ssh-keys}\n<!-- meta: domain=proxmox,network | severity=high | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-02-22 | keywords=ssh,proxmox_management,id_ed25519,gpu,pve,ssh-add,guest agent,596,agent fleet,ubuntu -->\n- SSH to **gpu** node works: `ssh -i ~/.ssh/proxmox_management root@192.168.1.67`\n- SSH to **pve** node is broken — use MCP for all operations\n- The `proxmox_management` key is the correct key for Proxmox hosts (not `id_ed25519`)\n- Guest agent exec endpoint (596 errors) can be intermittent — SSH is more reliable when available\n- **SSH agent keys don't persist across sessions** — after context compaction or new session, run `ssh-add ~/.ssh/id_ed25519` and `ssh-add ~/.ssh/proxmox_management` before SSH operations\n- Agent fleet VMs use `proxmox_management` key with user `ubuntu`: `ssh -i ~/.ssh/proxmox_management ubuntu@<IP>`\n\n### VM Cloning — Lock Files and ipconfig Pitfalls (2026-02-28) {#vm-cloning-lock-ipconfig}\n<!-- meta: domain=proxmox | severity=medium | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-02-28 | keywords=clone,lock,ipconfig1,cloud-init,qm unlock,qm set,cloudinit.qcow2,pvesm free,network-get-interfaces,stale disk,dual-NIC -->\n\nWhen cloning VMs in sequence on Proxmox 7.4:\n\n1. **Clone lock timeout**: After cloning, the VM may have `lock=clone` in its config AND a stale `/var/lock/qemu-server/lock-<vmid>.conf`. Both must be cleared before starting.\n   - API token cannot remove config locks (403 \"only root can set lock\")\n   - SSH to pve node works: `ssh -i ~/.ssh/proxmox_management root@192.168.1.66`\n   - Fix: `rm -f /var/lock/qemu-server/lock-<vmid>.conf && qm unlock <vmid>`\n\n2. **ipconfig1 from cloud-init**: When cloning a dual-NIC VM then removing net1, the Proxmox cloud-init config keeps `ipconfig1: ip=dhcp`. Cloud-init on the guest will wait indefinitely for the missing interface, blocking boot.\n   - Fix: `qm set <vmid> --delete ipconfig1` for all clones\n   - Apply to ALL clones immediately after creation, before booting\n\n3. **Stale disk volumes**: Previous failed clones leave cloudinit.qcow2 files on storage. Delete via API or `pvesm free <volid>` before retrying.\n\n4. **Finding VM IPs**: DHCP leases are on server subnet (192.168.1.x). Guest agent may not be ready yet — use ping sweep from edna-core (192.168.1.201) instead. `qm guest cmd <vmid> network-get-interfaces` is unreliable shortly after boot.\n\n### Proxmox API via curl — Token Auth Fails with `!` (2026-02-28) {#proxmox-api-curl-exclamation}\n<!-- meta: domain=proxmox,mcp | severity=medium | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-02-28 | keywords=curl,PVEAPIToken,401,zsh,history expansion,urllib,python,rename,qemu config,exclamation mark -->\n\nThe Proxmox API token ID `root@pam!claude-fleet` contains `!` which curl cannot send correctly even in single-quoted shell strings. The `!` gets escaped to `\\!` by zsh history expansion, causing 401 errors.\n\n**Fix**: Use Python `urllib` instead of curl for Proxmox API calls:\n```python\nimport urllib.request, urllib.parse, ssl, json\nctx = ssl.create_default_context()\nctx.check_hostname = False\nctx.verify_mode = ssl.CERT_NONE\ntoken = 'PVEAPIToken=root@pam!claude-fleet=999b2ef4-c70c-47bb-8385-61f931005344'\nreq = urllib.request.Request(url, data=data, method='PUT')\nreq.add_header('Authorization', token)\n```\n\n**Renaming VMs**: Use `PUT /nodes/{node}/qemu/{vmid}/config` with `name=<new-name>` body.\nThe MCP has no rename tool — Python urllib is the correct approach.\n\n### PVE Node Stuck — D-State Processes from Dead NFS (2026-03-01) {#pve-nfs-dstate-stuck}\n<!-- meta: domain=proxmox,storage,network | severity=critical | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-03-01 | keywords=D-state,NFS,pvestatd,sysrq,reboot,TrueNAS,uninterruptible sleep,load average,qm shutdown,soft mount,hard mount,proc/sysrq-trigger -->\n\nWhen TrueNAS (NFS server) dies while PVE has active NFS mounts, processes waiting on NFS enter D-state (uninterruptible sleep) and can't be killed even with SIGKILL. Key symptoms:\n- `pvestatd` stuck in D-state → VM starts fail with \"timeout waiting on systemd\"\n- `qm shutdown` and `qm stop` also fail (need systemd cooperation)\n- `reboot` command hangs (can't terminate D-state processes during shutdown)\n- Load average stays high (D-state processes count toward load)\n\n**Fix**: SysRq forced reboot is the only option:\n```bash\necho s > /proc/sysrq-trigger   # sync\nsleep 1\necho u > /proc/sysrq-trigger   # unmount\nsleep 1\necho b > /proc/sysrq-trigger   # reboot (immediate, no clean shutdown)\n```\n\n**Prevention**: Use `soft` NFS mount option instead of `hard` — soft mounts return errors instead of hanging forever. But PVE storage config manages mounts automatically.\n\n**Recovery sequence**: SysRq reboot → verify clean (no D-state, systemd running) → start TrueNAS first → verify NFS → start remaining VMs.\n\n### Claude Code Onboarding Wizard on Headless VMs (2026-03-02) {#claude-code-onboarding-headless}\n<!-- meta: domain=proxmox,discord | severity=medium | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-03-02 | keywords=claude.json,hasCompletedOnboarding,onboarding wizard,headless,browser,settings.local.json,fleet,theme -->\n\nClaude Code 2.1.63 shows an interactive onboarding wizard (theme + auth selection) on first run. On headless VMs this tries to open a browser, which fails. The wizard state is stored in `~/.claude.json`.\n\n**Fix**: Set these two keys in `~/.claude.json`:\n```python\npython3 -c \"\nimport json\nwith open('/home/ubuntu/.claude.json', 'r') as f: d = json.load(f)\nd['hasCompletedOnboarding'] = True\nd['theme'] = 'dark'\nwith open('/home/ubuntu/.claude.json', 'w') as f: json.dump(d, f, indent=2)\n\"\n```\n\n**Also**: `settings.local.json` in `~/.claude/` must exist (even empty `{\"permissions\":{\"allow\":[]}}`) or some wizard steps still trigger.\n\n**For fleet-wide fix**: Loop over all VM IPs and apply the same patch.\n\n### VM Static IPs via Netplan (2026-03-02) {#vm-static-ips-netplan}\n<!-- meta: domain=proxmox,network | severity=medium | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-03-02 | keywords=netplan,static IP,DHCP,50-cloud-init.yaml,netplan apply,ens18,ens19,OVS -->\n\nDHCP IPs change on reboot, breaking SSH aliases and scripts. Fix by writing static IPs directly in the VM's netplan config:\n```yaml\n# /etc/netplan/50-cloud-init.yaml\nnetwork:\n  version: 2\n  ethernets:\n    ens18:\n      dhcp4: false\n      addresses: [192.168.1.X/24]\n      routes: [{to: default, via: 192.168.1.254}]\n      nameservers: {addresses: [192.168.1.254, 8.8.8.8]}\n    ens19:\n      dhcp4: false\n      addresses: [192.168.0.X/24]\n```\nApply with `sudo netplan apply`. OVS warning is harmless.\n\n### exec claude --resume Kills SSH Sessions (2026-03-02) {#exec-claude-resume-kills-ssh}\n<!-- meta: domain=proxmox,discord | severity=high | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-03-02 | keywords=exec,claude,--resume,SSH,exit code 1,fallback,shell process,session ID -->\n\n`exec claude --resume` without a valid session ID exits immediately with code 1. Because `exec` replaces the shell process, the SSH session dies before any `|| fallback` can run. The `||` clause never executes — the shell is already gone.\n\n**Fix**: Don't use `exec` on the first attempt if you need a fallback:\n```bash\nclaude --resume 2>/dev/null || exec claude \"$@\"\n```\nOr just skip `--resume` entirely — CLAUDE.md and directives provide session context.\n\n### Fleet Auth: Env Var Overrides Credential File (2026-03-02) {#fleet-auth-env-var-override}\n<!-- meta: domain=proxmox,fleet | severity=critical | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-03-02 | keywords=CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN,/etc/claude-code.env,credentials.json,fleet-reauth,keychain,OAuth,expired token,env var override,fleet-health -->\n\n`CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` in `/etc/claude-code.env` overrides `~/.claude/.credentials.json`. When the env var token expires, Claude Code exits immediately — even if the credential file has a valid token.\n\n- All 7 fleet VMs had a stale OAuth token baked into `/etc/claude-code.env` that was sourced on every login shell\n- The credential file (deployed from Mac Keychain) was valid, but the env var took priority\n- **Fix**: Removed `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` from `/etc/claude-code.env` on all VMs. Auth now uses `~/.claude/.credentials.json` exclusively.\n- **Prevention**: Use `tools/fleet-reauth.sh` to deploy credentials from Mac Keychain. Use `tools/fleet-health.sh --fix` as cron (every 6h) for auto-healing.\n- **Rule**: Never hardcode OAuth tokens in env files — they expire. Use the credential file mechanism which supports refresh tokens.\n\n### Fleet Auth: NFS Symlinks Must Use Relative Paths (2026-03-02) {#nfs-symlinks-relative-paths}\n<!-- meta: domain=proxmox,storage | severity=high | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-03-02 | keywords=symlink,relative path,/Volumes/synologyNFS,/mnt/synology,checkpoint,latest,ln -sf,start-claude.sh,set -e -->\n\nNFS symlinks created on Mac use `/Volumes/synologyNFS/...` which doesn't exist on VMs (they mount at `/mnt/synology/...`). Absolute-path symlinks break cross-platform.\n\n- The checkpoint `latest` symlink pointed to `/Volumes/synologyNFS/server-manager/checkpoints/2026-03-02T09-50.md`\n- On VM 150, `head` on the broken symlink failed, and `set -euo pipefail` in `start-claude.sh` killed the script — session exited immediately after printing \"NFS synology: ok\"\n- **Fix**: Always use relative symlinks: `ln -sf 2026-03-02T09-50.md /Volumes/synologyNFS/server-manager/checkpoints/latest`\n- **Rule**: Any symlink on NFS must use a relative target (just the filename), never an absolute path.\n\n### Hardened VM Launch Scripts (2026-03-02) {#hardened-vm-launch-scripts}\n<!-- meta: domain=proxmox,fleet | severity=medium | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-03-02 | keywords=start-claude.sh,set -e,pipefail,non-fatal,best-effort,NFS mount,checkpoint,auth check,wrksplogin -->\n\nVM launch scripts (`start-claude.sh`) should only exit on auth failure. Everything else (NFS mounts, checkpoint loading, system info) should be best-effort.\n\n- `set -euo pipefail` caused silent exits when any non-critical command failed (broken symlink, missing file, etc.)\n- **Fix**: Removed `set -euo pipefail`. Only the auth check is fatal (`exit 1`). NFS mounts, checkpoint reads, and system info all use `|| true` or `2>/dev/null` fallbacks.\n- **Pattern**: For launch scripts, auth = fatal, everything else = best-effort with warnings.\n\n### Session Hook Pattern for Agent Fleet (2026-03-02) {#session-hook-pattern}\n<!-- meta: domain=proxmox,fleet | severity=high | tier=active | triggers=2 | last_triggered=2026-03-02 | keywords=session-start,sign-directive,directive signing,watchdog,NFS hooks,SessionStart,UserPromptSubmit,verify-directive -->\n- Implement `~/.claude/hooks/session-start.sh` (SessionStart hook) that verifies directive signatures via `verify-directive.sh` and loads latest directives from NFS into Claude context\n- Pair with `prompt-watchdog.sh` (UserPromptSubmit hook) that re-checks for directive changes mid-session\n- Agents always see latest directives without session restart; signature verification catches unauthorized edits\n- Watchdog ensures agents respond to orchestrator changes within the same session\n\n### LoRA Training on GPU Node: Three Config Pitfalls (2026-03-04) {#lora-training-config-pitfalls}\n<!-- meta: domain=proxmox,gpu | severity=high | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-03-04 | keywords=lora,accelerate,gpu_ids,shuffle_caption,cache_text_encoder,symlink,synology-main,train-queue,sd-scripts,1080ti -->\n- Three independent bugs caused all 8 LoRA training jobs to fail:\n  1. **`gpu_ids` YAML int**: `accelerate` config at `/root/.cache/huggingface/accelerate/default_config.yaml` parsed `gpu_ids: 0` as integer. Fix: quote it `gpu_ids: \"0\"`.\n  2. **Broken dataset symlinks**: Images symlinked to `/mnt/synology/...` but GPU node mounts at `/mnt/synology-main/...`. Fix: `find . -type l | while read link; do ... sed \"s|/mnt/synology/|/mnt/synology-main/|\" ...`.\n  3. **`shuffle_caption` + `cache_text_encoder_outputs`**: sd-scripts asserts these are mutually exclusive. Fix: set `shuffle_caption = false` in dataset template.\n- **Rule**: After any batch training failure, check the *first* character's error log — don't re-run the whole queue until you verify the fix. Each character fails differently when there are multiple bugs stacked.\n\n### Fleet Shared Context Pattern (2026-03-02) {#fleet-shared-context-pattern}\n<!-- meta: domain=proxmox,fleet | severity=medium | tier=active | triggers=7 | last_triggered=2026-03-02 | keywords=shared context,source material,agent fleet,drift prevention,parallel agents,spec document,phase 1,phase 2,orchestration -->\n- All agents must read the same shared source material (spec, vision doc, requirements) before producing role-specific outputs — prevents divergent assumptions across the fleet\n- Re-read the shared spec/vision document at the start of each session to prevent drift over time\n- Structure work in phases: Phase 1 (parallel reads of shared context → independent role-specific deliverables) then Phase 2 (deepening only after human review of Phase 1 outputs)\n- Assign distinct roles with non-overlapping output directories to avoid write conflicts\n- Specialist agents surface gaps in the shared spec that a single generalist would miss — collect these as structured questions for the human before Phase 2\n\n### EnterWorktree Tool (2026-02-21) {#enter-worktree-tool}\n<!-- meta: domain=proxmox | severity=low | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-02-21 | keywords=git worktree,EnterWorktree,.claude/worktrees,isolated,branch,experiment,prototyping -->\n- Creates a git worktree branched from HEAD inside `.claude/worktrees/`\n- Useful for isolated experiments without touching main working tree\n- On session exit, user gets prompted to keep or remove the worktree\n- Good for: config prototyping, infra-as-code branches, risky experiments\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":177,"oldLines":6,"newStart":177,"newLines":13,"lines":[" - Assign distinct roles with non-overlapping output directories to avoid write conflicts"," - Specialist agents surface gaps in the shared spec that a single generalist would miss — collect these as structured questions for the human before Phase 2"," ","+### Fleet Dispatch vs Local Subagents (2026-03-09) {#fleet-dispatch-vs-local}","+<!-- meta: domain=proxmox,fleet | severity=medium | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-03-09 | keywords=fleet dispatch,local agent,background agent,nod queue,SSH,VM ops,subagent -->","+- For ops tasks targeting specific VMs (media monitoring, service fixes, cron debugging), dispatch to the fleet via nod queue or direct SSH — not local background agents","+- Local subagents can't SSH into VMs, lack credentials, and duplicate work the orchestrator ends up doing anyway","+- **Rule**: Local subagents = research/analysis only. Fleet dispatch = anything that touches a VM or service","+- The fleet exists for a reason — use it","+"," ### EnterWorktree Tool (2026-02-21) {#enter-worktree-tool}"," <!-- meta: domain=proxmox | severity=low | tier=active | triggers=1 | last_triggered=2026-02-21 | keywords=git worktree,EnterWorktree,.claude/worktrees,isolated,branch,experiment,prototyping -->"," - Creates a git worktree branched from HEAD inside `.claude/worktrees/`"]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"7b0bf795-f3ac-418e-8804-75afe5444a98"}

Phase 1 (parallel) then Phase 2 (after human review). Non-overlapping output dirs.","newString":"- **Fleet shared context pattern** — all agents read shared source material before producing role-specific outputs; re-read spec each session to prevent drift. Phase 1 (parallel) then Phase 2 (after human review). Non-overlapping output dirs.\n- **Fleet dispatch vs local subagents** — use fleet (nod/SSH) for VM ops tasks, local subagents for research only. Local agents can't reach VMs and duplicate orchestrator work.","originalFile":"# Lessons Index\n\nOne-liner per lesson. Read this first, then fetch the relevant topic file.\n\n## MCP (`lessons/mcp.md`)\n- stdout pollution breaks MCP stdio transport — use console.error() only\n- Discord MCP token must be prefixed with \"Bot \" manually\n- Use fetch MCP for HTTP calls, not raw curl\n- **lgtv MCP**: `node --import tsx` fails in Claude Code — use `node_modules/.bin/tsx` binary directly\n- **grafana MCP**: env var is `GRAFANA_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_TOKEN`, not `GRAFANA_TOKEN`\n- **NFS-backed fleet reminders** — JSONL on NFS, shell scripts for create/resolve/check, integrated into session-start + watchdog hooks\n\n## Home Assistant (`lessons/ha.md`)\n- Cross-subnet discovery fails — HA can't find IoT devices without second NIC\n- HAOS won't boot with UEFI Secure Boot enabled (pre-enrolled-keys=1)\n- Proxmox API import-from silently fails on xz-compressed qcow2\n- HA REST API: use Bearer token with curl (fetch MCP lacks auth headers)\n- Integration pairing: all headless via config flow API except Plex (needs OAuth)\n- Cross-subnet fix: add net1 on vmbr1 to HA VM, no VLAN tag needed\n- Ollama subentries: use config subentries API for conversation agents\n- **Wyoming config flow**: REST API only — `POST /api/config/config_entries/flow` then submit host/port\n- **Config entry delete**: `DELETE /api/config/config_entries/entry/<id>` (WebSocket `config_entries/delete` = unknown_command)\n\n## Docker (`lessons/docker.md`)\n- **CRITICAL: NEVER deploy fresh containers over existing Plex config — back up Preferences.xml first, use tools/media-deploy.sh**\n- **CRITICAL: NEVER run `docker compose down -v` on media stack — `-v` deletes bind-mounted config data**\n- Plex MachineIdentifier is irreversible (SHA1 hash) — loss = all shared users disconnected permanently\n- Homepage config: write via Synology MCP, can't verify via fetch (Next.js SPA)\n- Portainer admin timeout: recreate container if init window missed\n- Uptime Kuma uses socket.io, not REST — must use web UI for initial setup\n- Container Manager port conflicts: DSM reserves 80/443, use alternates\n- **GPU node (192.168.1.67) ≠ media-server VM 200 (192.168.1.200)** — GPU workloads go to bare-metal GPU node, media stack is on VM 200 with NO GPU\n- Docker on ZFS: requires \"storage-driver\": \"zfs\" in daemon.json\n- WSL2 Docker IPv6 binding: ports bind to [::1] only, use v4tov6 portproxy\n- WSL2 + no-systemd: use /etc/wsl.conf [boot] command= to auto-start services\n- WSL2 from SSH: hangs in session 0 — use Windows Scheduled Task with /ru INTERACTIVE\n- WSL2 LxssManager stuck STOP_PENDING: use WMI Terminate (taskkill/Stop-Process fail even as admin)\n- **SLOW LESSON**: WSL over SSH is a trap — always use the Scheduled Task playbook from the start, never debug iteratively. See docker.md for full playbook.\n- **MusicGen/audiocraft Docker**: PyAV can't compile from source — use `--only-binary=av`. Never conda in pytorch images (upgrades torch). Use `--no-deps audiocraft` + manual deps. Pin torch ecosystem + `transformers==4.36.0` for torch 2.1.x.\n\n## Proxmox (`lessons/proxmox.md`)\n- LXC containers broken — always use QEMU VMs\n- SSH to pve node broken — use MCP; gpu node SSH works with proxmox_management key\n- SSH agent keys don't persist across sessions — run `ssh-add` before SSH ops\n- EnterWorktree tool: creates isolated git worktree in .claude/worktrees/\n- **Bulk VM ops (clone/resize/configure) take too long via MCP from Mac** — delegate to a VM agent with direct `qm` CLI access or SSH to PVE. 133 MCP calls = 16 min; same work via CLI = ~2 min.\n- **D-state from dead NFS blocks everything** — `pvestatd` stuck → VM starts fail, `reboot` hangs. Fix: SysRq `s/u/b` forced reboot. Prevention: use `soft` NFS mounts.\n- Clone locks: after `qm clone`, VMs get `lock=clone` in config. Need `qm unlock <vmid>` via SSH to PVE to clear.\n- ipconfig1 leftover: clones from dual-NIC VMs inherit `ipconfig1: ip=dhcp` — cloud-init hangs waiting for missing NIC. Fix: `qm set <vmid> --delete ipconfig1` before first boot.\n- **Claude Code onboarding wizard on headless VMs** — set `hasCompletedOnboarding: true` + `theme: \"dark\"` in `~/.claude.json` to skip browser-based wizard\n- **VM static IPs via netplan** — write static config to `/etc/netplan/50-cloud-init.yaml`, apply with `sudo netplan apply`\n- **`exec claude --resume` kills SSH** — `exec` replaces shell, so `|| fallback` never runs if claude exits. Don't exec the first attempt.\n- **Fleet auth env var override** — `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` in `/etc/claude-code.env` overrides credential file. Never hardcode OAuth tokens in env files; use `~/.claude/.credentials.json` + `fleet-reauth.sh`.\n- **NFS symlinks must be relative** — Mac creates `/Volumes/...` absolute symlinks that break on VMs (`/mnt/...`). Always `ln -sf <filename>` (relative).\n- **Hardened launch scripts** — `set -euo pipefail` in `start-claude.sh` kills sessions on any non-critical failure. Only auth should be fatal; NFS/checkpoint/info = best-effort.\n- **Session hook pattern** — SessionStart hook verifies directive signatures + loads latest from NFS; UserPromptSubmit watchdog re-checks mid-session. Agents always see current directives.\n- **LoRA training triple-bug** — accelerate `gpu_ids` must be string \"0\" not int, dataset symlinks must match actual NFS mount path, `shuffle_caption` conflicts with `cache_text_encoder_outputs`\n- **Fleet shared context pattern** — all agents read shared source material before producing role-specific outputs; re-read spec each session to prevent drift. Phase 1 (parallel) then Phase 2 (after human review). Non-overlapping output dirs.\n\n## Discord (`lessons/discord.md`)\n- Agent SDK nesting: bot refuses to spawn inside Claude Code session\n- Discord relay: use DM relay for physical pairing tasks (Apple TV PINs, etc.)\n- DAVE protocol: must install `@snazzah/davey` for discord.js voice to work\n- @discordjs/opus: CJS module, use default import in ESM projects\n- launchd EHOSTUNREACH: node under launchd can't reach IoT subnet — run from terminal as workaround\n- **macOS TCC blocks launchd from ~/Documents/** — move services to ~/opt/ or other non-protected dirs\n- .env with unquoted spaces breaks `source .env` under `set -e` — use dotenv in Node instead\n- **Midjourney via Discord relay** — `midjourney` npm package (erictik), user token auth, 1 concurrent + 10s delay, API-agnostic interface\n\n## Network (`lessons/network.md`)\n- UniFi MCP: basic read tools work, write tools don't — use web UI for config\n- HA → server subnet routing: HAOS default route goes through IoT interface\n- socat proxy on edna-core as routing workaround for cross-subnet\n- **Inter-VLAN TCP blocked** (ICMP OK): SynologyRouter blocks TCP between subnets, not UniFi. Fix: add vmbr1 NIC to VMs needing IoT access.\n\n## Voice / Edna (`lessons/voice.md`)\n- Guest agent exec: MCP blocks special chars, no shell pipes/redirects\n- Ollama needs $HOME set — guest agent exec doesn't set shell env\n- HA → Ollama: use IoT subnet IP (192.168.0.210), server subnet path hangs\n- Ollama Assist API: 163 entities makes prompt too large for 8B model on CPU\n- Cloud image clone: netplan has MAC-matched IPs, clone gets new MACs → no network\n- apt restricted repo: Ubuntu 22.04 has corrupt nvidia metadata, remove restricted\n- GPU Xid 79: GPU fell off PCIe bus, requires full node reboot\n- XTTS Wyoming: voice files in /data/voices/, scan_voices() only at container start\n- Kokoro GPU image `latest` dropped Pascal support (CUDA 12.9) — pin `v0.2.1` for 1080 Ti/1060\n- openWakeWord can't train full models on macOS (needs Piper/Linux) — use CoreWorxLab Docker trainer\n- Custom wake word: 16+ real voice samples → Kokoro TTS generates ~13K synthetic → train on GPU\n- Store training data on NFS for retraining (17GB ACAV100M features + AudioSet + FMA)\n- Kokoro TTS segfaults under WSL2 memory pressure — add circuit breaker, restart policy, shm_size\n- Trainer service needs `shm_size: '2g'` — PyTorch DataLoader workers crash with Bus error on Docker's default 64MB\n- CheckTraining scheduled task can reference stale container names — validate container is running, not just that logs exist\n- Writing files to WSL2 over SSH: scp to C:\\temp then wsl cp (pipes don't work through Windows CMD)\n- Phantom scheduled tasks: 37 leftover Oww* schtasks auto-launched trainers — always clean up after debugging\n- Trainer Dockerfile has no CMD: need `command:` in compose, not just `docker compose run trainer`\n- **Wyoming openWakeWord only loads TFLite** — `.onnx` files silently ignored (no onnxruntime in image)\n- **ONNX→TFLite**: use `onnx2tf` without `-oiqt` (INT8 fails on 3D tensor); float32 output works\n- **Wyoming HA integration**: use REST API for config flow (WebSocket lacks delete/flow commands)\n- **Wyoming protocol**: header JSON + raw data bytes (not a single JSON response)\n- **wake_word entity \"unknown\"**: normal state before first detection — not an error\n\n## Media / Indexers (`lessons/media.md`)\n- NZBGeek $12/yr: best value indexer, 11yr retention, unlimited API on paid tier, native Prowlarr def\n- Prowlarr MCP search returns partial results — Sonarr/Radarr query Prowlarr directly and work fine\n- Survivor S50E01: DMCA takedowns hit all usenet releases within hours — more indexers = better odds\n- SceneNZBs \"free\" API is actually a 30-day trial, not permanent\n- Tabula-Rasa registration window closes without warning — register immediately when open\n- **RECURRING**: Usenet auth failures — DON'T assume expired accounts. Check SABnzbd config against NFS secrets file (passwords may be wrong). Usenet Agency `482 Account expired` = generic auth rejection.\n- SABnzbd API: use `/api?mode=...` (not `/sabnzbd/api`), requires `apikey=` param\n- VM 200 at 94.7% RAM: guest agent dies, use SABnzbd HTTP API or Proxmox reboot as fallback\n- Radarr empty placeholder dirs are NOT files — RescanMovie won't help, use MoviesSearch to trigger downloads\n- Sonarr import blocked = check NAS dir ownership (UID mismatch prevents mkdir)\n- Radarr bulk import via API: lookup by TMDB, POST /api/v3/movie, RescanMovie to link files\n- VaultBoi1 (/movies) is read-only to Radarr — new downloads go to /movies2\n- macOS BSD grep lacks -P (PCRE) — use sed in scripts that run on Mac\n- **CRITICAL — Mardov creative adaptation**: DM is source material, not production manager. Players are primary audience. Ensemble — every player gets seen. Produce complete work → DM gives notes → iterate. Read `NORTH_STAR.md` before any Mardov work.\n- **LoRA training is wrong tool for early art direction** — lock visual style BEFORE training character models. LoRAs with bad training data + wrong base model = awful output. MJ `--cref`/`--sref` > LoRAs for MJ-primary pipeline. Be specific in style tags (name shows/studios, not \"Gothic anime\").\n- **Tiny WSL2 remote management** — SSH to port 2222 for direct Linux shell. Docker localhost broken, use container IP. OOM kills WSL entirely. WSL-Watchdog scheduled task for auto-recovery.\n- **Kling Playwright automation** — `storage_state` cookies don't persist auth; use `launch_persistent_context` with `~/.kling-profile/`. Login detection: \"Sign In\" text absence = logged in (Generate button visible even when logged out). Navigate to generation page for auth gate, not main feed. Videos use `blob:` URLs — intercept CDN URLs from API responses via `page.on(\"response\")`.\n- **CRITICAL — Kling i2v upload silently fails** — `input[type=\"file\"]` selector broke when Kling UI updated. Upload skips silently, Kling falls back to text-only generation. ALL 92 videos were generic t2v, not image-seeded. ALWAYS test one i2v manually before batch runs. Fix the selector first, verify the MJ source image appears in the UI, THEN batch.\n- **Kling download validation** — API 500 errors get saved as .mp4 files (399 bytes of JSON). Added validation: reject files <100KB + verify MP4 ftyp magic bytes. Always check `kling-old/` for backup copies before regenerating.\n- **MJ hopper websocket hang** — illustrator.js hangs indefinitely when MJ websocket drops silently. Added `timeout 900` wrapper. Monitor batch progress — if no log activity for 15+ min, the illustrator is stuck.\n- **CRITICAL — Discord selfbot websocket MESSAGE filtering** — Discord now suppresses MESSAGE_CREATE/MESSAGE_UPDATE events on selfbot gateway connections. MJ Imagine commands send fine (INTERACTION_SUCCESS) but results never arrive via websocket. Fix: HTTP poll `GET /channels/{id}/messages` every 5s. Deployed `illustrator-poll.js` on VM 150.\n- **CRITICAL — Kling URL capture grabs wrong videos** — `_extract_urls()` grabbed ANY .mp4 URL from ANY API response, including other users' videos in the feed gallery. All 92 downloads were random videos. Fix: capture task ID from `task/submit` response, only accept URLs from responses containing that task ID. Also: stale results file fallback in mj-hopper.sh read old batch-results — must compare against pre-existing file.\n- **MJ --no + --niji 7 conflict** — `--no` parameter is incompatible with `--niji 7` mode. Returns \"Invalid parameter\". Strip `--no` segments from niji prompts.\n\n## Gemini API / Model Routing (`lessons/media.md`)\n- **Gemini as coding agent**: keep tasks tiny (1 file change), send complete files not diffs, watch for truncation on 500+ line files\n- **gemini-code.sh pattern**: generate→apply→build→fix loop, auto-restores backups on failure\n- **Model availability**: 2.5 Flash works reliably; 2.0 Flash Lite quota takes hours to provision on new projects; 3.1 Flash Lite gets overloaded easily\n- **Import mutation**: Gemini sometimes changes unrelated imports (e.g. @dnd-kit/sortable → @dnd-kit/core) — always diff-review before committing\n\n## Storage / NAS (`lessons/storage.md`)\n- Synology MCP = FileStation only — query DSM API directly for storage health (SYNO.Storage.CGI.Storage)\n- Synology \"attention\" status = capacity warning (fs_almost_full), not disk failure\n- SHR pools spanning expansion units = eSATA SPOF — isolate each DX517 as its own pool\n- TrueNAS VM 104 uses physical passthrough via /dev/disk/by-id/ paths, not virtual disks\n- PVE hardware discovery: /nodes/pve/disks/list + /nodes/pve/hardware/pci via API token auth\n- TrueNAS Scratch degradation: ZFS metadata corruption from unclean shutdowns, not disk failure\n- **Persistent data must NOT live on Scratch** — moved to Synology SHR-2. Scratch = downloads/temp ONLY. Path mapping in `tool-guide.md`.\n\n## Hardware / USB (`lessons/hardware.md`)\n- USB 3.0 radiates 2.4GHz RF — interferes with wireless dongles (G733) and USB audio (Yeti)\n- Blue Yeti draws 500mA — passive USB hubs can't power it reliably\n- Fix: dedicated USB 2.0 hub physically separated from USB 3.0 ports\n- Corsair keyboards expose 3-4 HID endpoints — slow KVM re-enumeration, worse with iCUE running\n- Mac Mini has no USB 2.0 ports — need USB 2.0 hub via adapter for audio isolation\n- HA voice satellites: ESP32-S3-BOX-3 supports on-device microWakeWord, Voice PE has echo cancellation\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":56,"oldLines":6,"newStart":56,"newLines":7,"lines":[" - **Session hook pattern** — SessionStart hook verifies directive signatures + loads latest from NFS; UserPromptSubmit watchdog re-checks mid-session. Agents always see current directives."," - **LoRA training triple-bug** — accelerate `gpu_ids` must be string \"0\" not int, dataset symlinks must match actual NFS mount path, `shuffle_caption` conflicts with `cache_text_encoder_outputs`"," - **Fleet shared context pattern** — all agents read shared source material before producing role-specific outputs; re-read spec each session to prevent drift. Phase 1 (parallel) then Phase 2 (after human review). Non-overlapping output dirs.","+- **Fleet dispatch vs local subagents** — use fleet (nod/SSH) for VM ops tasks, local subagents for research only. Local agents can't reach VMs and duplicate orchestrator work."," "," ## Discord (`lessons/discord.md`)"," - Agent SDK nesting: bot refuses to spawn inside Claude Code session"]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"f37272df-5264-4241-96d0-fa9f3b6d1e55"}

Produced all 4 Phase 1 deliverables:\n    12→- `projects/mardov/lore/timeline.md` — ~700-line chronological event timeline (all 6 acts)\n    13→- `projects/mardov/lore/act-structure.md` — ~400-line narrative analysis with act breakdowns\n    14→- `projects/mardov/lore/characters.md` — Complete character bible (12 Zodiac PCs, 10+ other PCs, major NPCs, gods, group dynamics)\n    15→- `projects/mardov/lore/interview-questions-narrative.md` — 50 questions for DM Evie, grouped by topic, each with source context\n    16→\n    17→**Critical finding:** Acts 3-4 (Ch 42-102) have NO text Story.txt files — only image directories. This is ~45% of the campaign. The DM interview (Section I) is designed to fill this gap first.\n    18→\n    19→**Next steps for the project:** Evie interview → Phase 2 deliverables → Art direction pipeline.\n    20→\n    21→## 2026-03-02 ~11:35 — Costume Designer Phase 1 Complete (Mardov project)\n    22→Ran the Costume Designer agent session for Heroes of Mardov. Produced all 4 Phase 1 deliverables:\n    23→- `projects/mardov/production/costumes.md` — Per-character costume specs for 14 PCs + key NPCs (Default, Combat, Formal, Transformed, Signature Elements, Color Palette, Material Notes, Source Refs)\n    24→- `projects/mardov/production/costume-evolution.md` — Wardrobe evolution timeline across 6 acts with key costume moments and power visualization guide\n    25→- `projects/mardov/production/costume-reference-prompts.md` — FLUX Kontext generation-ready prompts with consistency anchors and negative prompt notes\n    26→- `projects/mardov/lore/interview-questions-costume.md` — 20 interview questions for DM Evie on wardrobe decisions\n    27→\n    28→**Key findings:**\n    29→- Nocturnus has a \"Cloak of Smoke\" (Brigit's Notes Ch 38) — one of the most visually distinctive items; needs origin story from Evie\n    30→- Nyx has a major hair color contradiction (backstory: purple; portrait: white/pink) — needs Evie's resolution\n    31→- Nightmare sword (Nocturnus) has a full corruption system with visual progression — hair loss, gauntness, madness\n    32→- 6 characters need eye color decisions before FLUX generation\n    33→- 3 characters (Dallor, Fen, Irethia) have incomplete base appearances\n    34→\n    35→**Blocking on:** 16 open questions requiring Evie's creative decisions (see interview-questions-costume.md)\n    36→\n    37→## 2026-03-02 ~22:00 — Color Scripter Phase 1 Complete (Mardov project)\n    38→Ran the Color Scripter agent session for Heroes of Mardov. Produced all 4 Phase 1 deliverables:\n    39→- `projects/mardov/production/color-emotion-map.md` — Universal color language covering 15 emotions, 20+ locations, and the four cosmological theme colors (War=forge-orange, Death=sickly-green, Pestilence=frost-blue, Famine=burnt-amber)\n    40→- `projects/mardov/production/color-scripts/act-1.md` through `act-6.md` — Scene-by-scene color progression for all 6 acts with dominant palette, accent colors, saturation, contrast, lighting direction, emotional color notes, and transition notes\n    41→- `projects/mardov/production/palette-reference-prompts.md` — FLUX Kontext mood board prompts organized by priority: act-level palettes, emotion extremes, location palettes, character palette tests\n    42→- `projects/mardov/lore/interview-questions-color.md` — 15 questions for DM Evie on instinctive color associations, emotional extremes, location mood, divine presence, and the Ch 103-107 gap\n    43→\n    44→**Key design decisions proposed:**\n    45→- The Raven Queen's \"candlelight against void\" motif as the series' visual signature (debuts in Act 1, recurs at every scale through divine apotheosis)\n    46→- Four cosmological theme colors (forge-orange/War, sickly-green/Death, frost-blue/Pestilence, burnt-amber/Famine) — introduced in the Tomb of the Last Curator, recur whenever their theme is invoked\n    47→- The party split (Act 3) as a literal PALETTE split — two color worlds running in parallel, each visually incomplete\n    48→- Bergs's apotheosis (Act 6) as the moment the color system BREAKS — a mortal character's palette becomes divine\n    49→- Asmodeus's death as a COLOR VOID — the Famine palette permanently loses its source\n    50→\n    51→**Confidence levels:** Acts 1-2 HIGH, Acts 3-4 LOW (narrative gap), Act 5 HIGH, Act 6 MEDIUM (campaign ongoing)\n    52→\n    53→**Dependencies consumed:** Art Director's visual-language.md, Director's episode-structure.md, Writer's Room's timeline.md and act-structure.md\n    54→\n    55→**Blocking on:** Evie's interview (especially Acts 3-4 emotional validation), campaign conclusion for Act 6 resolution, test shot generation to validate palettes\n    56→"}]},"uuid":"b12a6b1e-75f1-43dc-968e-4b5ffe42225a","timestamp":"2026-03-13T07:38:51.027Z"},"type":"agent_progress","prompt":"","agentId":"aac2084522ff9bd9e"},"toolUseID":"agent_msg_01Vyuc9bRNdFYXjsxAw7qGif","parentToolUseID":"toolu_01DSTRgYyMicdkJbiRZ4CPP6","uuid":"28356abe-3b21-47e9-b04c-395f6ebe3ced","timestamp":"2026-03-13T07:38:51.227Z"}
/Users/eviemini/.claude/projects/-Users-eviemini-Documents-code-server-manager/02610c2a-bd14-4804-96cd-a256bf0aa29c.jsonl:{"parentUuid":"764e3675-4b4e-4df7-9633-0f5335f5bf37","isSidechain":false,"userType":"external","cwd":"/Users/eviemini/Documents/code/server-manager","sessionId":"02610c2a-bd14-4804-96cd-a256bf0aa29c","version":"2.1.71","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"iterative-crafting-cat","type":"progress","data":{"message":{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01BEPBACpcZ3GJDyGFpeAD3m","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→---\n     2→name: MJ ban access, Kling image gen, Flux self-hosted, gothic anime LoRAs (2026)\n     3→description: Midjourney banned account web/image access, Kling Image 03 quality vs MJ, Flux.1 self-host GPU requirements vs API pricing, gothic/dark anime LoRAs for Illustrious XL and SDXL\n     4→type: reference\n     5→---\n     6→\n     7→# Midjourney Ban, Kling Image Gen, Flux Self-Host, Gothic LoRAs (2026)\n     8→\n     9→**Researched**: 2026-03-13\n    10→\n    11→---\n    12→\n    13→## 1. Midjourney: Official API / Web Access (Non-Discord)\n    14→\n    15→### Current state (March 2026)\n    16→- **No public official API.** Enterprise API is invite-only application form at `updates.midjourney.com/enterprise-api-survey/` — no release date, no public access.\n    17→- Web UI at midjourney.com IS the primary interface since 2024. Discord is now optional.\n    18→- Web UI features: inpainting editor, folder organize, bulk download, draft mode, batch jobs (2K items), personalize, explore.\n    19→- Web UI does NOT expose any programmatic interface or webhooks — it is SPA calling internal REST endpoints authenticated by session cookie.\n    20→- `igolaizola/midjourney-automation` (Apify actor) extracts session cookie and calls those internal endpoints — NOT Playwright/DOM scraping. Still TOS-violating, direct ban risk (your cookie = your account).\n    21→\n    22→### Can a banned Discord user access MJ through midjourney.com?\n    23→- **Almost certainly NO.** Account bans are platform-wide, not Discord-specific. The ban applies to the account (email/auth), not just Discord access.\n    24→- When banned: lose access immediately, no refund, no exceptions — confirmed by multiple user reports.\n    25→- Account deletion (voluntary or via ban) permanently deletes all images — MJ's own Data Deletion FAQ states account deletion removes all creations.\n    26→- **There is NO documented case of a banned account retaining read-only web gallery access.** All user reports indicate full lockout.\n    27→- Appeal path: contact support via midjourney.com or Discord. Generic denials are common; success rate is very low.\n    28→\n    29→---\n    30→\n    31→## 2. Midjourney: Image Export/Download After Ban\n    32→\n    33→### Bulk download (non-banned account)\n    34→- Web UI Organize section has bulk download — confirmed feature as of 2025.\n    35→- No API for automated download — requires manual action or browser automation (igolaizola actor can do bulk download as part of its pipeline, but this also violates TOS).\n    36→\n    37→### After ban\n    38→- **Images are inaccessible.** The ban locks the account, which controls access to the gallery.\n    39→- CDN URLs for individual images MAY remain accessible if you have the direct URL (e.g., `cdn.midjourney.com/...`) — CDN links from Discord attachments can persist separately from account status. But this is unreliable and undocumented.\n    40→- **If you have Discord job IDs** (from when you generated in Discord), you can try `/show <job_id>` from another Discord account in a MJ bot channel — but this requires another active MJ subscription.\n    41→- **Bottom line**: assume all images are lost when an account is banned. Mitigation: download everything with bulk export BEFORE any risky activity.\n    42→\n    43→### Preventive action\n    44→- Use the Organize > bulk download regularly as a backup habit.\n    45→- Discord attachment URLs are semi-permanent CDN links — can be downloaded separately via script.\n    46→- Do NOT use annual billing if running automation — ban = no refund.\n    47→\n    48→---\n    49→\n    50→## 3. Kling AI Image Generation\n    51→\n    52→### What is it\n    53→- Kuaishou (China) — primarily known for video AI, but now has dedicated image models:\n    54→  - **Kling Image 01**: December 2025 — first dedicated image model\n    55→  - **Kling Image 03 (3.0)**: February 4, 2026 — current flagship\n    56→\n    57→### Kling Image 03 technical capabilities\n    58→- Visual Chain-of-Thought reasoning: reasons through scene composition before rendering (lighting logic, spatial relationships, mirror physics)\n    59→- Diffusion Transformer + Deep-Stack architecture\n    60→- Native 2K/4K generation (not upscaled — actual diffusion at high res)\n    61→- Up to 10 reference images per generation\n    62→- Image Series Mode: 2-9 sequential images with consistent style + narrative\n    63→- Dual-reward reinforcement learning: balances photorealism and cinematic aesthetics\n    64→- Understands cinematography language: \"low angle,\" \"85mm f/1.4,\" \"dutch tilt\" etc.\n    65→- Intelligent aspect ratio detection from reference images\n    66→\n    67→### Kling Image quality vs Midjourney (anime/illustration)\n    68→- **Kling Image 03 is photorealism-first, cinematic-first** — not optimized for anime/illustration flat art\n    69→- **Midjourney (Niji 7)** produces more visually striking illustrative/stylized output for anime\n    70→- For anime-style webtoon: MJ Niji 7 > Kling Image 03\n    71→- For photorealistic manga (live-action cinematic style): Kling Image 03 competitive\n    72→- For consistency in character-driven sequences: Kling Image Series Mode is interesting but benchmarks show subtle drift across 5+ panels\n    73→- Multi-reference input (10 images) is a real advantage for character consistency — more capable than MJ which has no --cref in Niji 7\n    74→- **Verdict**: Kling is NOT a direct MJ replacement for gothic anime/webtoon. Use it for: image-to-video generation from self-hosted SD outputs, or where cinematic composition language matters more than flat illustration style.\n    75→\n    76→### Kling API pricing (images)\n    77→- Subscription plans: Standard $10/mo (660 credits, ~3,300 images at 0.2 credits each), Pro $37/mo (3,000 credits)\n    78→- API credits: variable by output size and model version — 0.2 credits for standard image\n    79→- At Pro tier (~$37/mo, 3,000 credits): ~15,000 standard images/mo = $0.0025/image\n    80→- At Standard tier (~$10/mo, 660 credits): ~3,300 images/mo = $0.003/image\n    81→- For 600 images: Standard plan covers it easily, ~$10/mo\n    82→- Direct API available at `klingai.com/global/dev/`\n    83→\n    84→---\n    85→\n    86→## 4. Best Self-Hosted Models for Gothic Anime / Webtoon at Scale (600+ images)\n    87→\n    88→### Model rankings for gothic anime / dark webtoon style\n    89→\n    90→| Model | VRAM needed | Style fit | Speed (RTX 3090) | Commercial use |\n    91→|-------|-------------|-----------|-------------------|----------------|\n    92→| **Illustrious XL 2.0** | 8GB (medvram) / 12GB full | Excellent anime | ~200 img/hr @1024px | MIT-like, check Civitai terms |\n    93→| **FLUX.1-dev** | 12-16GB (Q8/FP8) / 24GB (BF16) | Excellent, photorealistic-leaning | ~15-20s/img (RTX 3090) | Non-commercial (Apache for schnell) |\n    94→| **FLUX.1-schnell** | 8GB (Q4) | Good, faster | 1-4 steps = 3-5s/img | Apache 2.0 — commercial OK |\n    95→| **FLUX.2 [klein] 4B** | ~8GB | Good | Sub-second (Jan 2026) | Check BFL licensing |\n    96→| **NovelAI Diffusion v3** | 8-12GB | Best for pure anime | Similar to Illustrious | Proprietary, no commercial |\n    97→\n    98→**Winner for gothic anime webtoon at scale: Illustrious XL 2.0 + LoRA stack in ComfyUI**\n    99→\n   100→### VRAM requirements by approach\n   101→\n   102→| GPU | VRAM | What runs |\n   103→|-----|------|-----------|\n   104→| GTX 1080 Ti | 11GB | Illustrious XL with `--medvram`; FLUX schnell Q4; FLUX Kontext FP4 GGUF (7GB). Use cu121 PyTorch — NOT cu128. |\n   105→| RTX 3080 | 10-12GB | Same as above + FLUX dev FP8 borderline |\n   106→| RTX 3090 | 24GB | Everything: Illustrious XL full, FLUX dev BF16, FLUX Kontext full |\n   107→| RTX 4090 | 24GB | Same as 3090, ~2x faster |\n   108→\n   109→### Gothic anime LoRAs available on Civitai (Illustrious XL / SDXL)\n   110→\n   111→| LoRA | Platform | Style | Notes |\n   112→|------|----------|-------|-------|\n   113→| **Dark Anime Style** | Illustrious | Dark anime, high contrast | Updated Nov 2025, Illustrious-native |\n   114→| **Gothic Detail Enhancer** | SDXL/Illustrious/Pony | Gothic atmosphere enhancer | Works across checkpoint families |\n   115→| **Gothic Anime Style XL** | SDXL | Gothic anime character art | v1.0, older but works |\n   116→| **Dark Gothic Fantasy** | SDXL | Gothic + fantasy themes, backgrounds | Good for environmental shots |\n   117→| **Darkness/暗闇化 (Hotaru)** | Illustrious/Pony | Dark atmosphere technique | Characters bright on dark BG, dramatic |\n   118→| **Goth/Emo Girl** | FLUX + SDXL | Character style | Available for both FLUX and SDXL |\n   119→| **Anime High Contrast** | Illustrious | High-contrast manga look | Good for panel-style art |\n   120→| **Manhwa Artstyle** | Illustrious/SDXL | Korean webtoon/manhwa | Civitai 257995, trigger: `manhwa-artstyle` |\n   121→\n   122→**Recommended stacking for gothic webtoon:**\n   123→- Base: Illustrious XL 2.0\n   124→- Style LoRA: `Manhwa Artstyle` (0.4-0.5) + `Dark Anime Style` (0.3-0.4)\n   125→- Atmosphere LoRA: `Gothic Detail Enhancer` (0.3) OR `Darkness/暗闇化` (0.4)\n   126→- Character LoRA: custom-trained per character (0.6-0.7)\n   127→- Do NOT stack more than 3-4 LoRAs simultaneously — quality degrades\n   128→\n   129→### Generation time estimates at 20 steps, DPM++ 2M Karras\n   130→\n   131→| GPU | Resolution | Time/image | Images/hr |\n   132→|-----|-----------|------------|-----------|\n   133→| GTX 1080 Ti (medvram) | 1024x1024 | ~45-60s | ~70-80 |\n   134→| RTX 3090 | 1024x1024 | ~15-18s | ~200-220 |\n   135→| RTX 3090 | 1024x1536 (webtoon) | ~20-25s | ~160-180 |\n   136→| RTX 4090 | 1024x1024 | ~7-10s | ~400-500 |\n   137→\n   138→For 600 images on a single RTX 3090: ~3-4 hours batch run.\n   139→\n   140→---\n   141→\n   142→## 5. Flux.1 Pro: Self-Hosted vs API\n   143→\n   144→### Can Flux be self-hosted?\n   145→\n   146→**Yes. FLUX.1-dev and FLUX.1-schnell are open weights from Black Forest Labs.**\n   147→- `black-forest-labs/FLUX.1-dev` — Apache 2.0 for non-commercial, commercial license available\n   148→- `black-forest-labs/FLUX.1-schnell` — Apache 2.0 (commercial OK)\n   149→- FLUX.2 models (klein, max) — newer, check per-model licensing\n   150→\n   151→### VRAM requirements (self-hosted Flux.1)\n   152→\n   153→| Quantization | VRAM | Notes |\n   154→|-------------|------|-------|\n   155→| BF16 (full) | 24GB | RTX 3090/4090 only |\n   156→| FP8 | ~12-13GB | RTX 3080 Ti, 3090 at lower VRAM |\n   157→| Q8 | ~13GB | Similar to FP8 |\n   158→| Q6-Q4 | 8-11GB | RTX 3080, 4070. Noticeable quality drop at Q4 |\n   159→| FP4 GGUF (Kontext) | 7GB | Quantized Kontext — 97.2% vs BF16 quality |\n   160→\n   161→- RTX 3090 (24GB): runs FLUX.1-dev at full BF16, ~12-15s/image\n   162→- GTX 1080 Ti (11GB): runs Q6/Q4 quantized only — not recommended for FLUX full model. Illustrious XL is better use of this GPU.\n   163→\n   164→### FLUX.1-schnell vs dev\n   165→\n   166→- **Schnell**: 1-4 steps, ~3-5s/img on RTX 3090, Apache 2.0 (commercial OK), quality slightly lower\n   167→- **Dev**: 20-30 steps, ~12-20s/img on RTX 3090, non-commercial weights, higher quality\n   168→- Quality gap: dev wins on coherence and detail; schnell is usable and fast\n   169→\n   170→### FLUX.2 [klein] — newest (January 2026)\n   171→- 4B parameter model, fits ~8GB VRAM\n   172→- Sub-second generation on consumer GPUs (RTX 3090/4070+)\n   173→- Still checking licensing status\n   174→\n   175→### API pricing comparison\n   176→\n   177→| Provider | Model | Price/image (1024x1024) |\n   178→|----------|-------|------------------------|\n   179→| **bfl.ai (Black Forest Labs)** | FLUX.2 Pro | $0.03/megapixel → $0.03 at 1MP |\n   180→| **fal.ai** | FLUX.2 Pro | $0.03/image (1MP) |\n   181→| **fal.ai** | FLUX.1-dev | ~$0.025-0.035/image |\n   182→| **Replicate** | FLUX.1-dev | ~$0.055/image (GPU time based) |\n   183→| **fal.ai** | FLUX.1-schnell | ~$0.003-0.005/image |\n   184→\n   185→### Self-hosted vs API cost break-even\n   186→\n   187→For 600 images at $0.03/image (fal.ai FLUX.2 Pro): $18 total.\n   188→\n   189→Self-hosted electricity + hardware amortization:\n   190→- RTX 3090 running FLUX.1-dev: 600 images × 15s = 2.5hr GPU time\n   191→- At 350W TDP: 2.5hr × 0.35kW = 0.875 kWh ≈ $0.10-0.15 electricity\n   192→- If you own the GPU: self-hosting wins by far at scale.\n   193→- If you need to rent cloud GPU: RunPod RTX 3090 at ~$0.44/hr × 2.5hr = ~$1.10 vs $18 API — self-host on cloud wins too.\n   194→\n   195→**Verdict: For 600+ images on owned hardware, self-host always wins on cost. API wins for convenience/no-setup.**\n   196→\n   197→---\n   198→\n   199→## Recommendation Summary\n   200→\n   201→**For gothic anime webtoon at 600+ images:**\n   202→1. **Primary**: Self-hosted Illustrious XL 2.0 in ComfyUI on RTX 3090 (or RTX 3080 Ti). Layer `Manhwa Artstyle` + `Dark Anime Style` + `Gothic Detail Enhancer` LoRAs. Use ControlNet (AnimeLineArt + ZoeDepth) for structural consistency. Train per-character LoRAs. ~200 img/hr.\n   203→2. **Fallback API**: fal.ai FLUX.1-schnell (~$0.003-0.005/img) for rapid iteration without GPU overhead.\n   204→3. **Avoid**: Kling Image for anime/illustration style — it's photorealism-first. Use Kling for image-to-video conversion if needed.\n   205→4. **MJ ban recovery**: If banned, assume images are gone. The ban is account-wide, not Discord-specific. Act before the ban: bulk-export from Organize tab regularly. CDN URLs from Discord DMs may survive short-term.\n   206→\n   207→---\n   208→\n   209→## Sources\n   210→- https://apatero.com/blog/midjourney-banned-account-alternatives-2025 (MJ ban behavior, Produced all 4 Phase 1 deliverables:\n- `projects/mardov/lore/timeline.md` — ~700-line chronological event timeline (all 6 acts)\n- `projects/mardov/lore/act-structure.md` — ~400-line narrative analysis with act breakdowns\n- `projects/mardov/lore/characters.md` — Complete character bible (12 Zodiac PCs, 10+ other PCs, major NPCs, gods, group dynamics)\n- `projects/mardov/lore/interview-questions-narrative.md` — 50 questions for DM Evie, grouped by topic, each with source context\n\n**Critical finding:** Acts 3-4 (Ch 42-102) have NO text Story.txt files — only image directories. This is ~45% of the campaign. The DM interview (Section I) is designed to fill this gap first.\n\n**Next steps for the project:** Evie interview → Phase 2 deliverables → Art direction pipeline.\n\n## 2026-03-02 ~11:35 — Costume Designer Phase 1 Complete (Mardov project)\nRan the Costume Designer agent session for Heroes of Mardov. Produced all 4 Phase 1 deliverables:\n- `projects/mardov/production/costumes.md` — Per-character costume specs for 14 PCs + key NPCs (Default, Combat, Formal, Transformed, Signature Elements, Color Palette, Material Notes, Source Refs)\n- `projects/mardov/production/costume-evolution.md` — Wardrobe evolution timeline across 6 acts with key costume moments and power visualization guide\n- `projects/mardov/production/costume-reference-prompts.md` — FLUX Kontext generation-ready prompts with consistency anchors and negative prompt notes\n- `projects/mardov/lore/interview-questions-costume.md` — 20 interview questions for DM Evie on wardrobe decisions\n\n**Key findings:**\n- Nocturnus has a \"Cloak of Smoke\" (Brigit's Notes Ch 38) — one of the most visually distinctive items; needs origin story from Evie\n- Nyx has a major hair color contradiction (backstory: purple; portrait: white/pink) — needs Evie's resolution\n- Nightmare sword (Nocturnus) has a full corruption system with visual progression — hair loss, gauntness, madness\n- 6 characters need eye color decisions before FLUX generation\n- 3 characters (Dallor, Fen, Irethia) have incomplete base appearances\n\n**Blocking on:** 16 open questions requiring Evie's creative decisions (see interview-questions-costume.md)\n\n## 2026-03-02 ~22:00 — Color Scripter Phase 1 Complete (Mardov project)\nRan the Color Scripter agent session for Heroes of Mardov. Produced all 4 Phase 1 deliverables:\n- `projects/mardov/production/color-emotion-map.md` — Universal color language covering 15 emotions, 20+ locations, and the four cosmological theme colors (War=forge-orange, Death=sickly-green, Pestilence=frost-blue, Famine=burnt-amber)\n- `projects/mardov/production/color-scripts/act-1.md` through `act-6.md` — Scene-by-scene color progression for all 6 acts with dominant palette, accent colors, saturation, contrast, lighting direction, emotional color notes, and transition notes\n- `projects/mardov/production/palette-reference-prompts.md` — FLUX Kontext mood board prompts organized by priority: act-level palettes, emotion extremes, location palettes, character palette tests\n- `projects/mardov/lore/interview-questions-color.md` — 15 questions for DM Evie on instinctive color associations, emotional extremes, location mood, divine presence, and the Ch 103-107 gap\n\n**Key design decisions proposed:**\n- The Raven Queen's \"candlelight against void\" motif as the series' visual signature (debuts in Act 1, recurs at every scale through divine apotheosis)\n- Four cosmological theme colors (forge-orange/War, sickly-green/Death, frost-blue/Pestilence, burnt-amber/Famine) — introduced in the Tomb of the Last Curator, recur whenever their theme is invoked\n- The party split (Act 3) as a literal PALETTE split — two color worlds running in parallel, each visually incomplete\n- Bergs's apotheosis (Act 6) as the moment the color system BREAKS — a mortal character's palette becomes divine\n- Asmodeus's death as a COLOR VOID — /Users/eviemini/.claude/projects/-Users-eviemini-Documents-code-server-manager/02610c2a-bd14-4804-96cd-a256bf0aa29c.jsonl:{"parentUuid":"3bbae540-16e9-4ebe-8a45-d17a779959a0","isSidechain":false,"userType":"external","cwd":"/Users/eviemini/Documents/code/server-manager","sessionId":"02610c2a-bd14-4804-96cd-a256bf0aa29c","version":"2.1.71","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"iterative-crafting-cat","type":"progress","data":{"message":{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_019chApK9SchtMDYTGyAJ82K","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# Research Agent Memory\n     2→\n     3→## Key Findings (cross-session)\n     4→\n     5→### AI Music Generation for Cinematic Score Hopper (2026)\n     6→- See `ai-music-generation-2026.md` for full breakdown, comparison table, and hopper architecture\n     7→- **Suno v5**: best quality, 3rd-party REST API (APIPASS/AIMLAPI ~$0.02-0.05/track), stems on Premier, commercial on paid plan. No official API.\n     8→- **MusicGen (Meta)**: best for automation — open source Python API, self-host on 1080 Ti (cu121), free. `cog-musicgen-looper` for seamless loops. CC-BY-NC-4.0 weights (non-commercial unless licensed from Meta).\n     9→- **Mubert**: only service with real developer REST API + native seamless looping. $49/mo. Weak for orchestral scoring, strong for ambient BGM loops.\n    10→- **AIVA**: best pure orchestral quality + MIDI export, but NO public API — enterprise contact only. Use semi-manually for leitmotif creation.\n    11→- **Stable Audio 2.5**: Replicate API (~$0.0023/sec), audio continuation. Needs 16GB VRAM to self-host (11GB on 1080 Ti is borderline).\n    12→- **UDIO: AVOID** — post-UMG settlement is a walled garden, no downloads/exports at all.\n    13→- Character themes: pin prompt template per character (Suno) or use MusicGen melody conditioning (reference chromagram).\n    14→- Stems from any generated audio: LALAL.AI or AudioShake API as post-processing step.\n    15→\n    16→### TTS Models for Voice Cloning on GTX 1080 Ti (Pascal, 11GB VRAM)\n    17→- See `tts-models-2026.md` for full model-by-model breakdown\n    18→- See `chatterbox-docker-deployment.md` for full Chatterbox Docker + Wyoming deployment\n    19→- Best fit: **F5-TTS** (2GB VRAM, zero-shot cloning, warmest quality, non-streaming)\n    20→- Runner-up: **Chatterbox Turbo** (~4-6GB VRAM, near-ElevenLabs quality, emotion control)\n    21→- Ultra-low latency: **CosyVoice 2** (150ms streaming, 0.5B, Apache 2.0)\n    22→- AVOID: Fish Speech S1 (12GB min), Orpheus 3B (15GB), Qwen3-TTS 1.7B (16GB)\n    23→- Kokoro has best Wyoming integration but NO voice cloning support\n    24→- Wyoming bridge: `roryeckel/wyoming_openai` bridges any OpenAI-compatible TTS to Wyoming\n    25→- wyoming_openai WAV bug: null bytes / \"invalid RIFF start code\" = WAV header in PCM stream. Fix: use `TTS_STREAMING_MODELS`, request `pcm` format, or build custom server with Python `wave` module. See chatterbox-docker-deployment.md.\n    26→- wyoming-satellite is DEPRECATED (2025). Use `OHF-Voice/linux-voice-assistant` (ESPHome protocol) for Pi satellites\n    27→- Custom \"Hey Edna\" wake word: train with openWakeWord Colab (~1hr), drop .tflite in /share/openwakeword/. English only.\n    28→- Post-processing quick win: pedalboard LowShelfFilter +5dB @ 400Hz adds warmth to XTTS v2\n    29→\n    30→### PyTorch + Pascal (sm_61, GTX 1080 Ti) Compatibility — CRITICAL\n    31→- Pascal = CUDA compute capability 6.1 (sm_61)\n    32→- PyTorch 2.8+ cu128 DROPS Pascal support (confirmed PyTorch dev team, July 2025)\n    33→- SAFE wheels: cu121 (torch 2.5.1), cu124 (torch 2.6.0), cu126 (torch 2.9.0+cu126 still works)\n    34→- DO NOT use: cu128 or cu130 — \"no kernel image available\" error on 1080 Ti\n    35→- devnen/Chatterbox-TTS-Server: use `requirements-nvidia.txt` (cu121), NOT `docker-compose-cu128.yml`\n    36→- travisvn/chatterbox-tts-api: uses cu124 base image — Pascal-safe out of the box\n    37→\n    38→### Sonos TTS + HA audioclip on Home Theater Groups (Arc S19 surround)\n    39→- See `sonos-tts-audioclip.md` for full breakdown\n    40→- Root causes for silent TTS on Sonos Arc 5.1 surround group: (1) TCP port 1443 blocked on IoT subnet, (2) audioclip API limitation — only targets the coordinator/primary speaker in bonded group, Arc gets the call but Era 300 surrounds are silent or nothing plays at all\n    41→- audioclip is S2-only (Arc is S2, safe) but home theater bonded groups have known silent-failure behavior\n    42→- Direct `media_player.play_media` with URL works = confirms HA can reach Sonos on port 8123, but port 1443 from HA to Sonos may still be blocked\n    43→- Best workaround: `sonos.snapshot` + `media_player.play_media` (announce: false, no audioclip path) + `sonos.restore`\n    44→- Alternative: snapshot/restore automation using Chime TTS (bypasses audioclip entirely)\n    45→- Do NOT use `tts.speak` directly with home theater groups — it always uses audioclip\n    46→- IP ban: expired TTS URLs can cause Sonos to hammer HA and trigger `ip_ban_enabled` (default: true); check HA logs for 403s from Sonos\n    47→\n    48→### Plex Server Identity Recovery\n    49→- See `plex-server-identity.md` for full breakdown\n    50→- ProcessedMachineIdentifier = SHA1(\"<MachineIdentifier>- Plex Media Server\") — confirmed from plexinc/pms-docker source\n    51→- SHA1 is one-way: cannot reverse a ProcessedMachineIdentifier to get the original UUID\n    52→- plex.tv API NEVER exposes raw MachineIdentifier UUID — only ProcessedMachineIdentifier (called `clientIdentifier` or `machineIdentifier` confusingly)\n    53→- Can set ProcessedMachineIdentifier directly in Preferences.xml — Plex uses it as-is if present\n    54→- Per Plex staff ChuckPa (July 2025): once server deleted from plex.tv, identity is permanently dead\n    55→- Check if record still exists: `curl \"plex.tv/api/servers/<ProcessedMachineIdentifier>?X-Plex-Token=...\"` — 200 = alive, 404 = gone\n    56→- Having PlexOnlineToken + ProcessedMachineIdentifier often sufficient to reconnect (raw UUID not needed by plex.tv)\n    57→\n    58→### Free Newznab Usenet Indexers for Prowlarr (2025-2026)\n    59→- See `usenet-indexers-newznab-2026.md` for full table and notes\n    60→- Best free open-registration: **NZBNoob** (100 API/day, 5 NZB, lifetime free)\n    61→- Register NOW: **Tabula-Rasa** (open Feb 2026, closes sporadically — 25 API/day free)\n    62→- Best free API count: **SceneNZBs** (2000 API/day free BUT short ~1yr retention, German scene focus)\n    63→- AVOID free tier: altHUB (no API after 14d trial), ABNZB (view-only), NZBgeek free (~20 API/day, useless)\n    64→- NZBgeek/NZBFinder worth paying: $12/yr and $15/yr respectively for real automation limits\n    65→- DOGnzb invite-only; nzb.cat invite-only; NZBPlanet API disabled on free tier\n    66→\n    67→### openWakeWord Custom Model Training (macOS Apple Silicon)\n    68→- See `openwakeword-training-macos.md` for full step-by-step breakdown\n    69→- macOS: ONLY `onnxruntime` installs (no tflite-runtime) — always use `inference_framework=\"onnx\"`\n    70→- Full custom \"Hey Edna\" model: ONLY via Google Colab or Linux/CUDA Docker — Piper TTS has NO macOS wheels\n    71→- Colab path: https://colab.research.google.com/drive/1q1oe2zOyZp7UsB3jJiQ1IFn8z5YfjwEb (~1hr, free T4)\n    72→- Upload real WAVs to Google Drive, set `custom_positive_clips_dir`, they get 3x weight over synthetic\n    73→- Colab exports both `.tflite` (for HA /share/openwakeword/) and `.onnx` (for local macOS testing)\n    74→- Verifier model (train_custom_verifier): works on macOS NOW, but only personalizes a pre-trained phrase, does NOT add new phrases\n    75→- Real-time detection: chunk size = 1280 samples (80ms), needs ~2s warmup, default threshold 0.5\n    76→- CoreWorxLab trainer (Kokoro TTS, Docker, NVIDIA): good local option, run on GTX 1080 Ti node with cu121\n    77→\n    78→### ESP32-S3 Voice Satellites for Home Assistant (2025-2026)\n    79→- See `esp32-s3-voice-satellites.md` for full hardware breakdown and BOM\n    80→- Official HA starter board: **M5Stack ATOM Echo** (~$14-22 USD) — runs openWakeWord via server\n    81→- Best pre-built: **HA Voice Preview Edition** ($58.95 USD, ameriDroid) — ESP32-S3 + XMOS XU316 DSP, dual mics, built-in speaker, echo cancellation. NO custom wake word support out-of-the-box (microWakeWord only, predefined models).\n    82→- Mid-range: **ESP32-S3-BOX-3** (~$53.90 USD, Adafruit/DigiKey) — display, dual mics, speaker, openWakeWord or microWakeWord. Good mid-tier.\n    83→- DIY build: ESP32-S3-DevKitC (N16R8) + INMP441 mic + MAX98357A amp + speaker: ~$15-25 AliExpress total\n    84→- microWakeWord v2 official pre-trained models: `okay_nabu`, `alexa`, `hey_jarvis`, `hey_mycroft` only\n    85→- Custom wake word \"Hey Edna\" (3 syllables — good): use openWakeWord Colab (~1hr) for server-side, OR microWakeWord Apple Silicon trainer (TaterTotterson) for on-device\n    86→- TaterTotterson/microWakeWords: community repo, request custom model via GitHub issue, maintainer trains and publishes .tflite\n    87→- ATOM Echo custom wake word: openWakeWord path — train .tflite, drop in /share/openwakeword/, assign to assistant\n    88→- HA Voice PE custom wake word: technically possible via ESPHome firmware rebuild (microWakeWord), but buggy per community (May 2025)\n    89→- Room count: 1 device per room you want voice control; whole house = 1 per bedroom + kitchen + living room (typically 4-6 devices)\n    90→\n    91→### Secure Torrent Stack (qBittorrent + gluetun + *arr, 2026)\n    92→- See `torrent-vpn-stack-2026.md` for full architecture, VPN comparison, and security checklist\n    93→- Best VPN for Docker torrenting with port forwarding: **AirVPN** (persistent ports, gluetun WireGuard, ~€1.79/mo 3yr)\n    94→- Runner-up: **PIA** (court-proven no-logs), but WireGuard port forwarding BROKEN in gluetun — use OpenVPN\n    95→- Avoid Mullvad for torrenting: removed port forwarding May 2023\n    96→- gluetun pattern: qBittorrent uses `network_mode: \"service:gluetun\"` — shares network namespace, automatic kill switch\n    97→- PIA WireGuard + gluetun port forwarding: open bug as of Jan/Dec 2025 — use OpenVPN with PIA\n    98→- AirVPN + gluetun port forwarding: works but can drop after tunnel restart — add healthcheck-triggered restarts\n    99→- qBittorrent network interface: bind to `wg0` (WireGuard) or `tun0` (OpenVPN) in Advanced tab\n   100→- Anonymous Mode: ON for public trackers, OFF for private trackers (breaks user-agent auth)\n   101→- Best TV private tracker: BTN (invite-only); fallback: TorrentLeech + BeyondHD\n   102→- Sonarr hostname for qBit in Docker: use `gluetun:8080` (not `qbittorrent:8080`)\n   103→- Unified path structure avoids remote path mapping: /data/torrents/, /data/usenet/, /data/media/\n   104→\n   105→### Discord Voice Bot (STT + LLM + TTS) with @discordjs/voice\n   106→- See `discord-voice-bot-2026.md` for full architecture, code patterns, and challenge log\n   107→- Recommended library: **@discordjs/voice** (stays in existing TypeScript/discord.js v14 codebase)\n   108→- Voice receive works: `receiver.subscribe(userId, { end: { behavior: EndBehaviorType.AfterSilence, duration: 1000 } })` returns Opus stream\n   109→- Opus decode to PCM: pipe through `prism-media` opus.Decoder (48kHz stereo 16-bit) then ffmpeg to 16kHz mono WAV for Whisper\n   110→- TTS playback: `createAudioResource(wavStream)` — ffmpeg handles 24kHz->48kHz resampling automatically when using StreamType.Arbitrary\n   111→- Known bug: VoiceReceiver stops capturing after ~5 min of inactivity. Workaround: re-subscribe on each speaking event, don't keep subscription open\n   112→- Known bug: `speaking` event does NOT fire while bot is playing audio — must stop/pause player before listening, or use separate connection\n   113→- Interruption: call `player.stop()` when new Opus data arrives mid-playback; track `isSpeaking` flag per user\n   114→- Latency budget: VAD silence tail 500-1000ms + Groq STT ~200ms + Claude Haiku first-token ~500ms + Chatterbox TTS ~1-2s + playback start = ~3-4s total\n   115→- Turn-taking: use `AfterSilence: 800ms` as VAD, ignore utterances < 0.3s (noise filter), only respond if transcript non-empty\n   116→- Whisper hallucinations on silence: filter common false positives (\"Thank you\", \"you\", etc.) or set minimum audio duration threshold\n   117→- Required deps: `@discordjs/voice`, `@discordjs/opus`, `prism-media`, `ffmpeg-static`, `libsodium-wrappers`\n   118→- GatewayIntentBits needed: add `GuildVoiceStates` to existing intents in client.ts\n   119→- Bot permissions needed: Connect, Speak, Use Voice Activity (NOT deafened — join with `selfDeaf: false`)\n   120→\n   121→### NSFW Image Classifier on macOS Apple Silicon (2026)\n   122→- See `nsfw-classifier-macos-2026.md` for full tool-by-tool breakdown\n   123→- Best for batch photo library (10K+): **NudeNet v3** (onnxruntime, granular labels, adjustable threshold) or **Falconsai/nsfw_image_detection** (ViT, 98% accuracy, ~52 img/s, Apache 2.0)\n   124→- NudeNet key advantage: 18 granular body-part classes — filter to EXPLICIT_CLASSES set to eliminate swimwear false positives\n   125→- Falconsai: binary safe/nsfw, MPS-accelerated via PyTorch on Apple Silicon, most popular HF NSFW model\n   126→- AVOID: SD Safety Checker (too many false positives), nsfwjs (tfjs-node arm64 build issues, slow for batch), Apple Vision (no built-in NSFW API)\n   127→- opennsfw2: MIT, Yahoo model, good but higher false positive rate on suggestive-but-clothed images; TF Metal optional\n   128→- Secure storage: encrypted sparse bundle (`hdiutil create -type SPARSEBUNDLE -encryption AES-256`) for macOS-only; VeraCrypt for cross-platform\n   129→- `printf '%s' \"$PASSWORD\" | hdiutil create ... -stdinpass` for non-interactive scripting\n   130→\n   131→### Claude Max Plan Rate Limit Scheduling (Fleet/Automation)\n   132→- See `claude-max-rate-limit-scheduling.md` for full details\n   133→- Usage API: `GET https://api.anthropic.com/api/oauth/usage` with `anthropic-beta: oauth-2025-04-20` — returns `five_hour.utilization` (%) and `five_hour.resets_at` (ISO 8601)\n   134→- Linux token: `cat ~/.claude/.credentials.json | jq -r '.claudeAiOauth.accessToken'` (expires ~6h; use `claude setup-token` for persistent)\n   135→- Error message in interactive mode: `\"Claude usage limit reached. Your limit will reset at 2pm (America/New_York)\"`\n   136→- JSONL log event: `{\"type\": \"rate_limit_event\", \"rate_limit_info\": {\"status\": \"rejected\", ...}}`\n   137→- No dedicated exit code for rate limits — parse output text\n   138→- Best fleet tool: **ralph-claude-code** (3-layer detection, auto-wait, circuit breaker) for autonomous loops\n   139→- Best scheduler with quota awareness: **claude-tasks** (kylemclaren) — skips jobs when utilization > threshold\n   140→- Key gotcha: all claude surfaces (claude.ai + CLI + Desktop) share the same 5h quota pool\n   141→\n   142→### Anime Style Transfer for Webtoon/Motion Comic Production (2026)\n   143→- See `anime-style-transfer-webtoon-2026.md` for full pipeline breakdown and ranked options\n   144→- Best pipeline: **Illustrious XL
